{"parentUuid":"4414faa9-a4f8-4535-8487-2d50db19df33","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"acb4f19ad404af610","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- heartbeat-protocol: Claude node contract for DebateAI Graph Spine v2. The Main Orchestrator (Claude-Router seat) contract; thin loader over the repo spine. All agent launches use /goal.\n- commit-commands:clean_gone: Cleans up all git branches marked as [gone] (branches that have been deleted on the remote but still exist locally), including removing associated worktrees.\n- commit-commands:commit-push-pr: Commit, push, and open a PR\n- commit-commands:commit: Create a git commit\n- pdf-viewer:annotate: Collaboratively annotate a PDF — propose markup, review together, iterate\n- pdf-viewer:fill-form: Fill PDF form fields interactively with live visual feedback\n- pdf-viewer:open: Open a PDF in the interactive viewer\n- pdf-viewer:sign: Place a signature or initials image on a PDF\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- mattpocock-skills:diagnosing-bugs: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Use when the user says \"diagnose\"/\"debug this\", or reports something broken/throwing/failing/slow.\n- mattpocock-skills:tdd: Test-driven development. Use when the user wants to build features or fix bugs test-first, mentions \"red-green-refactor\", or wants integration tests.\n- mattpocock-skills:prototype: Build a throwaway prototype to answer a design question. Use when the user wants to sanity-check whether a state model or logic feels right, or explore what a UI should look like.\n- mattpocock-skills:research: Investigate a question against high-trust primary sources and capture the findings as a Markdown file in the repo. Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent.\n- mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when the user wants to pin down domain terminology or a ubiquitous language, record an architectural decision, or when another skill needs to maintain the domain model.\n- mattpocock-skills:codebase-design: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary.\n- mattpocock-skills:code-review: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \"review since X\".\n- mattpocock-skills:resolving-merge-conflicts: Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\n- mattpocock-skills:grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\n- superpowers:brainstorming: You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation.\n- superpowers:dispatching-parallel-agents: Use when facing 2+ independent tasks that can be worked on without shared state or sequential dependencies\n- superpowers:executing-plans: Use when you have a written implementation plan to execute in a separate session with review checkpoints\n- superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch: Use when implementation is complete, all tests pass, and you need to decide how to integrate the work\n- superpowers:receiving-code-review: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\n- superpowers:requesting-code-review: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n- superpowers:subagent-driven-development: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n- superpowers:systematic-debugging: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n- superpowers:test-driven-development: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n- superpowers:using-git-worktrees: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback\n- superpowers:using-superpowers: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions\n- superpowers:verification-before-completion: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n- superpowers:writing-plans: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code\n- superpowers:writing-skills: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment\n- pdf-viewer:view-pdf: Interactive PDF viewer. Use when the user wants to open, show, or view a PDF and collaborate on it visually — annotate, highlight, stamp, fill form fields, place signature/initials, or review markup together. Not for summarization or text extraction (use native Read instead).\n- productivity:memory-management: Two-tier memory system that makes Claude a true workplace collaborator. Decodes shorthand, acronyms, nicknames, and internal language so Claude understands requests like a colleague would. CLAUDE.md for working memory, memory/ directory for the full knowledge base.\n- productivity:start: Initialize the productivity system and open the dashboard. Use when setting up the plugin for the first time, bootstrapping working memory from your existing task list, or decoding the shorthand (nicknames, acronyms, project codenames) you use in your todos.\n- productivity:task-management: Simple task management using a shared TASKS.md file. Reference this when the user asks about their tasks, wants to add/complete tasks, or needs help tracking commitments.\n- productivity:update: Sync tasks and refresh memory from your current activity. Use when pulling new assignments from your project tracker into TASKS.md, triaging stale or overdue tasks, filling memory gaps for unknown people or projects, or running a comprehensive scan to catch todos buried in chat and email.\n- cowork-plugin-management:cowork-plugin-customizer: Customize a Claude Code plugin for a specific organization's tools and workflows. Use when: customize plugin, set up plugin, configure plugin, tailor plugin, adjust plugin settings, customize plugin connectors, customize plugin skill, tweak plugin, modify plugin configuration.\n- cowork-plugin-management:create-cowork-plugin: Guide users through creating a new plugin from scratch in a cowork session. Use when users want to create a plugin, build a plugin, make a new plugin, develop a plugin, scaffold a plugin, start a plugin from scratch, or design a plugin. This skill requires Cowork mode with access to the outputs directory for delivering the final .plugin file.\n- engineering:architecture: Create or evaluate an architecture decision record (ADR). Use when choosing between technologies (e.g., Kafka vs SQS), documenting a design decision with trade-offs and consequences, reviewing a system design proposal, or designing a new component from requirements and constraints.\n- engineering:code-review: Review code changes for security, performance, and correctness. Trigger with a PR URL or diff, \"review this before I merge\", \"is this code safe?\", or when checking a change for N+1 queries, injection risks, missing edge cases, or error handling gaps.\n- engineering:debug: Structured debugging session — reproduce, isolate, diagnose, and fix. Trigger with an error message or stack trace, \"this works in staging but not prod\", \"something broke after the deploy\", or when behavior diverges from expected and the cause isn't obvious.\n- engineering:deploy-checklist: Pre-deployment verification checklist. Use when about to ship a release, deploying a change with database migrations or feature flags, verifying CI status and approvals before going to production, or documenting rollback triggers ahead of time.\n- engineering:documentation: Write and maintain technical documentation. Trigger with \"write docs for\", \"document this\", \"create a README\", \"write a runbook\", \"onboarding guide\", or when the user needs help with any form of technical writing — API docs, architecture docs, or operational runbooks.\n- engineering:incident-response: Run an incident response workflow — triage, communicate, and write postmortem. Trigger with \"we have an incident\", \"production is down\", an alert that needs severity assessment, a status update mid-incident, or when writing a blameless postmortem after resolution.\n- engineering:standup: Generate a standup update from recent activity. Use when preparing for daily standup, summarizing yesterday's commits and PRs and ticket moves, formatting work into yesterday/today/blockers, or structuring a few rough notes into a shareable update.\n- engineering:system-design: Design systems, services, and architectures. Trigger with \"design a system for\", \"how should we architect\", \"system design for\", \"what's the right architecture for\", or when the user needs help with API design, data modeling, or service boundaries.\n- engineering:tech-debt: Identify, categorize, and prioritize technical debt. Trigger with \"tech debt\", \"technical debt audit\", \"what should we refactor\", \"code health\", or when the user asks about code quality, refactoring priorities, or maintenance backlog.\n- engineering:testing-strategy: Design test strategies and test plans. Trigger with \"how should we test\", \"test strategy for\", \"write tests for\", \"test plan\", \"what tests do we need\", or when the user needs help with testing approaches, coverage, or test architecture.\n- design:accessibility-review: Run a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit on a design or page. Trigger with \"audit accessibility\", \"check a11y\", \"is this accessible?\", or when reviewing a design for color contrast, keyboard navigation, touch target size, or screen reader behavior before handoff.\n- design:design-critique: Get structured design feedback on usability, hierarchy, and consistency. Trigger with \"review this design\", \"critique this mockup\", \"what do you think of this screen?\", or when sharing a Figma link or screenshot for feedback at any stage from exploration to final polish.\n- design:design-handoff: Generate developer handoff specs from a design. Use when a design is ready for engineering and needs a spec sheet covering layout, design tokens, component props, interaction states, responsive breakpoints, edge cases, and animation details.\n- design:design-system: Audit, document, or extend your design system. Use when checking for naming inconsistencies or hardcoded values across components, writing documentation for a component's variants, states, and accessibility notes, or designing a new pattern that fits the existing system.\n- design:research-synthesis: Synthesize user research into themes, insights, and recommendations. Use when you have interview transcripts, survey results, usability test notes, support tickets, or NPS responses that need to be distilled into patterns, user segments, and prioritized next steps.\n- design:user-research: Plan, conduct, and synthesize user research. Trigger with \"user research plan\", \"interview guide\", \"usability test\", \"survey design\", \"research questions\", or when the user needs help with any aspect of understanding their users through research.\n- design:ux-copy: Write or review UX copy — microcopy, error messages, empty states, CTAs. Trigger with \"write copy for\", \"what should this button say?\", \"review this error message\", or when naming a CTA, wording a confirmation dialog, filling an empty state, or writing onboarding text.\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:explain-usage: Explain where this session's tokens went, with one simple chart in plain language. Use when the user says things like \"explain my usage\", \"where did my tokens go\", or asks for a usage breakdown.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email or summary); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user mentions \"deck,\" \"slides,\" \"presentation,\" or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. If a .pptx or .potx file needs to be opened, created, or touched, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:schedule: Create or update a scheduled task that runs automatically. Use when the user says things like \"every day\", \"each morning\", \"remind me in an hour\", \"run this at noon\", or wants to reschedule an existing task.\n- anthropic-skills:setup-cowork: Guided Cowork setup — install role-matched plugins, connect your tools, try a skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts — when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: IMPORTANT: These instructions OVERRIDE any default behavior and you MUST follow them exactly as written.\n\nContents of /Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3/CLAUDE.md (project instructions, checked into the codebase):\n\n# DebateAI-V3 — Agent Field Manual (Root)\n\nLiving conventions for humans and AI agents. Keep it short, exact, and current.\n\n## Documentation Discipline (READ FIRST)\n\n- **Cite, don't restate:** Point to the canonical doc + AC/DR id. Never paraphrase binding text.\n- **One fact, one home:** Requirements in `requirements-spec.md`; rulings in `agent-protocols/decisions-log.md`; C4 in `architecture/c4-model.md`. No forking.\n- **AC-first:** Every change traces to an AC or DR id. No id → not started.\n- **RATIFIED > agent memory:** On conflict, canonical docs + decisions-log win. Update memory, not the doc.\n- **Frozen mission:** `docs/missions/2026-08-06-v3-programming/` is scope-frozen. New scope → new mission.\n\n## The 12 Design Patterns (P-register — RATIFIED, DR-125)\n\nCanonical: `docs/agent-protocols/design-patterns.md`. Enforced in review. **DR-115 ABSOLUTE.**\n\n- **P1 Serve-only-verified:** Gate reads (attempts.status='pass') ∧ (evidence exists) ∧ (checksum matches). One gate, whole fleet.\n- **P2 Deterministic-clean-first:** Deterministic pre-clean before any judge call. Same input → same output.\n- **P3 Attempt-ledger-append-only:** Every verify attempt appends a row. **No deletes, no updates. DR-115.**\n- **P4 Envelope-frozen-at-run-start (AC-50):** Compute once; read from run record forever after.\n- **P5 Tier-authority-resolved (P8 chain):** parent → run → deployment. **Never a source literal.**\n- **P6 Budget-from-ledger (ADR-0017):** Derive from attempts ledger. Never an independent counter.\n- **P7 Honesty-surfaces-unattached:** Test-only/later-slice → UNATTACHED + neverCalled. Never falsely ATTACHED.\n- **P8 Strategy+registry-resolution:** parent → run → deployment chain. Fail loud if unresolved. No default tier.\n- **P9 Checksum-verified-serve:** No serve without checksum match.\n- **P10 Append-only-everywhere:** Ledgers, evidence, audit logs. **DR-115 ABSOLUTE.**\n- **P11 Typed-loud-failures:** No silent catch. No invented numbers (AC-76). Fail with typed errors.\n- **P12 Registry-resolution-chain:** All resolution via registry. No hardcoded lookups.\n\n## Non-Negotiables\n\n1. **DR-115 ABSOLUTE:** Append-only everywhere. No deletes/updates to ledgers, evidence, audit logs. Violation = automatic reject.\n2. **AC-76 No invented numbers:** Every numeric traces to a register row or ruled doc. Else typed-loud failure.\n3. **No orphaned scope:** Every surface attaches to a real caller, or is marked UNATTACHED + neverCalled. (P7)\n4. **Frozen basis:** Envelope basis pinned at run start (AC-50/P4). Never re-derived mid-run.\n5. **Tier via chain:** parent → run → deployment (P8). Never a source literal. Fail loud if unresolved.\n\n## Stack (RATIFIED — DR-117, DR-118)\n\n- **Language:** TypeScript (strict) on Node ≥ 20. **DR-117.**\n- **Orchestration:** Hatchet (durable task runner). **DR-118.**\n- **DB:** PostgreSQL 18 (embedded-postgres for tests — real DB, no mocks).\n- **Test:** vitest. Behavioral, real-DB where it matters. RED before green.\n- **Migrations:** Plain SQL, replay-safe, forward-only. Lint-checked.\n\n## Commands\n\n- Test: `./node_modules/.bin/vitest run tests/unit tests/integration tests/architecture`\n- Typecheck: `pnpm run typecheck`\n- Lint: `pnpm run lint`\n- One test: `./node_modules/.bin/vitest run tests/unit/<file>.test.ts`\n\n## Review Law (dual-greenlight)\n\nTwo independent reviewers (Claude-lane + Grok-lane). Both must greenlight. Either rejection → back to sticky worker. Verdicts cite AC/DR ids. Adversarial but convergent. See `docs/agent-protocols/review-protocol.md`.\n\nThe user opened this skill, which means they want to use it. {"parentUuid":"086c421c-ddd5-4190-b3d5-698c535bca0f","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"0ebabfcd-4e56-44ce-a82f-49f11a444d76","agentId":"acb4f19ad404af610","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /Users/vladmihaimiron/Library/Application Support/Claude/local-agent-mode-sessions/eca2b00e-5445-4ba9-8142-77f809823448/bde69b8f-e4d7-4fa8-a7fe-cf85d310038e/rpm/plugin_01FTLa86dhbVJ3HB1LdHdhN7/skills/code-review\n\n# /code-review\n\n> If you see unfamiliar placeholders or need to check which tools are connected, see [CONNECTORS.md](../../CONNECTORS.md).\n\nReview code changes with a structured lens on security, performance, correctness, and maintainability.\n\n## Usage\n\n```\n/code-review <PR URL or file path>\n```\n\nReview the provided code changes: @$1\n\nIf no specific file or URL is provided, ask what to review.\n\n## How It Works\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                      CODE REVIEW                                   │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│  STANDALONE (always works)                                       │\n│  ✓ Paste a diff, PR URL, or point to files                      │\n│  ✓ Security audit (OWASP top 10, injection, auth)               │\n│  ✓ Performance review (N+1, memory leaks, complexity)           │\n│  ✓ Correctness (edge cases, error handling, race conditions)    │\n│  ✓ Style (naming, structure, readability)                        │\n│  ✓ Actionable suggestions with code examples                    │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│  SUPERCHARGED (when you connect your tools)                      │\n│  + Source control: Pull PR diff automatically                    │\n│  + Project tracker: Link findings to tickets                     │\n│  + Knowledge base: Check against team coding standards           │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n## Review Dimensions\n\n### Security\n- SQL injection, XSS, CSRF\n- Authentication and authorization flaws\n- Secrets or credentials in code\n- Insecure deserialization\n- Path traversal\n- SSRF\n\n### Performance\n- N+1 queries\n- Unnecessary memory allocations\n- Algorithmic complexity (O(n²) in hot paths)\n- Missing database indexes\n- Unbounded queries or loops\n- Resource leaks\n\n### Correctness\n- Edge cases (empty input, null, overflow)\n- Race conditions and concurrency issues\n- Error handling and propagation\n- Off-by-one errors\n- Type safety\n\n### Maintainability\n- Naming clarity\n- Single responsibility\n- Duplication\n- Test coverage\n- Documentation for non-obvious logic\n\n## Output\n\n```markdown\n## Code Review: [PR title or file]\n\n### Summary\n[1-2 sentence overview of the changes and overall quality]\n\n### Critical Issues\n| # | File | Line | Issue | Severity |\n|---|------|------|-------|----------|\n| 1 | [file] | [line] | [description] | 🔴 Critical |\n\n### Suggestions\n| # | File | Line | Suggestion | Category |\n|---|------|------|------------|----------|\n| 1 | [file] | [line] | [description] | Performance |\n\n### What Looks Good\n- [Positive observations]\n\n### Verdict\n[Approve / Request Changes / Needs Discussion]\n```\n\n## If Connectors Available\n\nIf **~~source control** is connected:\n- Pull the PR diff automatically from the URL\n- Check CI status and test results\n\nIf **~~project tracker** is connected:\n- Link findings to related tickets\n- Verify the PR addresses the stated requirements\n\nIf **~~knowledge base** is connected:\n- Check changes against team coding standards and style guides\n\n## Tips\n\n1. **Provide context** — \"This is a hot path\" or \"This handles PII\" helps me focus.\n2. **Specify concerns** — \"Focus on security\" narrows the review.\n3. Clarified on the record: this is one-time design-time\n34\tconfig, fully automatic at runtime, no human in any user's loop. Until ratified,\n35\trows behave as correctness (never skipped).\"*\n36\t\n37\t**Row inventory.** The **exact 71-row identity space of PRE-05**\n38\t(`docs/architecture/10-row-contracts.md` §6, §7.1) — Q1…Q62 and R1…R9, with\n39\tPRE-05's `MACHINE` / `HYBRID` / `LLM` labels carried unchanged. **One inventory,\n40\ttwo documents**: PRE-05 says *when* a row runs, this document says *whether it may\n41\tbe shed for money*. No id, label or count is minted here.\n42\t\n43\t**Reviewers on handoff:** Codex + Grok (DR-101's maker-diversity diamond for a\n44\tClaude-authored ticket).\n45\t\n46\t**Revision 2 — what the fix cycle changed.** Grok APPROVED rev 1; Codex returned\n47\tCHANGES REQUESTED with four findings, all four accepted and fixed. **No row\n48\tchanged class**, the protected-core map is unchanged, and the checksum\n49\t(69 / 2 · 13 / 57 / 1 · 71) is unchanged.\n50\t\n51\t| Finding | Change |\n52\t|---|---|\n53\t| **1 · Q49's basis was labelled RULED; it is `[CD]`** | §4.9 Q49 and **CP-1** now state that T-2 rests on **C-6 / C-7, both `CARRIED-DESIGN`**, that DR-031 ratified only the MACHINE classification, and that no later DR promotes them. Carried onto the surface as **rider (d)**. §3.1 now records the evidence grade of every conjunct as a standing discipline. |\n54\t| **2 · The \"only Q27 satisfies `FX-SRV-16`\" claim was not established** | New **§5.1** quotes `FX-SRV-16` in full: the mark is **answer-scoped with an affected-node set**, so \"node-scoped vs answer-scoped\" was the wrong discriminator and **the uniqueness claim is withdrawn**. §5.2 re-grounds both rows on **scope of work**, §5.4 restates the brittleness accurately (the existence condition is discharged **twice**), and **SP-C1** hands the affected-set derivation to S9/S5 rather than assuming it. |\n55\t| **3 · §4 labels were not PRE-05 verbatim** | All **71** `Row · label` cells now copied **verbatim** from `10-row-contracts.md` §6 (machine-diffed to zero mismatches); the POLICY_BLOCKED annotations on Q14 / Q40 / R6 moved out of the identity cell into the Ground column. |\n56\t| **4 · The no-number attestation was false** | §1 and §9 narrowed to *\"invents no numeric value; every numeric value that appears is quoted with its source\"*, with the two-item inventory named. Q45's paraphrase (*\"nearly ten times\"*) replaced by the spec's verbatim figures. |\n57\t\n58\t---\n59\t\n60\t## 1. What this document is, and what it is not\n61\t\n62\t**What it is.** The classification spec §21.2 requirement **N-14** demands:\n63\t*\"Which rows are correctness and which are enrichment must be classified **per\n64\trow, once**, and the classification is part of the row's contract rather than an\n65\toperational setting.\"* All 71 rows, each with the option it adopts and a\n66\tone-line justification tied to the protected core and to the charter's needs.\n67\t\n68\t**What it is not.**\n69\t\n70\t- **It is not an activation decision.** A **correctness** row can still file\n71\t  `INACTIVE` when its predicate says so (PRE-05 §6) — that is the row not being\n72\t  *owed*, which is a different thing from the row being *shed for cost*. The two\n73\t  fields are orthogonal and both are per-row contract fields.\n74\t- **It is not a runtime setting.** DR-093 on the record: **one-time design-time\n75\t  config, fully automatic at runtime, no human in any user's loop.** Nothing here\n76\t  puts a person in a user's path.\n77\t- **It invents no numeric value** (AC-76 · DR-039). **Every numeric value that\n78\t  appears is quoted with its source named**, and the inventory is short enough to\n79\t  check: **DR-050's `K=1`** deepening bound (§2, quoting spec §10.2 C-5a) and\n80\t  **the spec's worked composition case** (§4.9 Q45, quoted verbatim from spec §3.9\n81\t  Q45). Nothing else numeric appears except **counts of rows**, each checked\n82\t  against PRE-05 §7.1 and spec §3.13's checksum. *(This attestation was narrowed\n83\t  in rev 2: the earlier \"no cap appears\" wording was false against §2's own\n84\t  quotation.)*\n85\t- **It does not decide the envelope's values.** N-9's envelope is derived from\n86\t  asker depth × risk tier; those are register rows, and they are V's at their own\n87\t  step.\n88\t- **It does not rule the questions it finds.** §6 lists them for V.\n89\t\n90\t---\n91\t\n92\t## 2. The law this classification instantiates\n93\t\n94\tQuoted, because every filing in §4 appeals to it.\n95\t\n96\t**DR-021 knob 9** (founding ledger): *\"budget override = **TYPED SKIP FOR\n97\tENRICHMENT ONLY** — correctness/safety rows (provenance, abstention typing,\n98\tstandard+ blind verification, citation routes) can never be budget-skipped; every\n99\tskip carries a visible **SKIPPED-BY-BUDGET** marker.\"*\n100\t\n101\t**DR-052** (founding ledger): *\"exhaustion → **typed enrichment skips first**,\n102\tthen hard stop serving already-verified components with `ENVELOPE_EXHAUSTED` —\n103\tnever a silent timeout. **PROTECTED CORE (never skippable): provenance,\n104\tabstention typing, standard+ blind verification, citation routes, AND\n105\tserve-conformance (added).**\"*\n106\t\n107\t**Spec §21.2 N-11 — the protected core** `RULED(DR-052)`: *\"**Never skippable,\n108\twhatever the envelope says:** provenance · abstention typing ·\n109\tstandard-and-above blind verification · citation routes · **serve-conformance**.\n110\tThe last is the addition that matters: conformance is the only machine\n111\tenforcement standing between a model's prose and a reader, and without naming it,\n112\tbudget law permitted skipping it and recording a legal `SKIPPED-BY-BUDGET` while\n113\tthe serving philosophy quietly stopped operating.\"*\n114\t\n115\t**Spec §21.2 N-12 — orthogonality** `RULED(DR-052)`: *\"The knob classifies\n116\t**which rows** may be skipped; the envelope bounds **the whole run's product**\n117\tand provides a terminal. Neither substitutes for the other.\"* **This document is\n118\tthe knob's half only.**\n119\t\n120\t**Charter §5.2 row 6 — BLOCKING(DR-063)**: *\"An enrichment row skipped under the\n121\tenvelope serves with a visible SKIPPED-BY-BUDGET mark; a protected-core row\n122\trefuses the skip.\"* Authority: RATIFIED(DR-021 knob 9, DR-052). Fixture\n123\t`FX-C52-06`, slice **S9**, launch-readiness dependency **LRD-1**.\n124\t\n125\t**Two riders the classification must not break:**\n126\t\n127\t- **`SKIPPED-BY-BUDGET` is itself honesty surface 8** (spec §12, the nine\n128\t  surfaces, `RULED(DR-048)`). An enrichment skip is therefore never a silence: it\n129\t  is a served, typed mark. That is the whole reason a row *can* be enrichment.\n130\t- **Cost may not terminate a correctness loop.** Spec §10.2 C-5a states it in\n131\t  terms: *\"because a correctness row may never be budget-skipped (§19 H8), **cost\n132\t  could not terminate the loop either**\"* — which is why DR-050's K=1 bound\n133\t  exists. A classification that tried to make an expensive loop terminate by\n134\t  calling it enrichment would be re-litigating a ruled bound.\n135\t\n136\t---\n137\t\n138\t## 3. The standing option text — the two options every row chooses between\n139\t\n140\t**DR-061's pattern:** V ratifies **by reference to the option text recorded\n141\there**. Because every row faces the *same* binary, the option text is stated once\n142\tand each row in §4 adopts **(a)** or **(b)** by reference to it.\n143\t\n144\t> **Option (a) · CORRECTNESS — never budget-skippable.** The row is owed whenever\n145\t> its predicate says it is owed, and the envelope may not shed it. A run short of\n146\t> envelope takes N-10's other limb instead: the **hard stop that serves the\n147\t> already-verified components with `ENVELOPE_EXHAUSTED`**. Cost of choosing (a):\n148\t> the envelope binds sooner on big runs.\n149\t>\n150\t> **Option (b) · ENRICHMENT — skippable, loudly.** Under envelope pressure the\n151\t> row may be skipped **first** (N-10's ordering), and the skip serves as a visible\n152\t> **`SKIPPED-BY-BUDGET`** mark — an **answer-scoped condition mark whose affected\n153\t> set is stored once and projected per node at read time** (`FX-SRV-16`; see\n154\t> §5.1). Cost of choosing (b): the row's contribution goes dark on exactly the\n155\t> biggest, most expensive runs.\n156\t\n157\t### 3.1 The test applied to every row — three conjuncts, all required for (b)\n158\t\n159\tA row is proposed **enrichment** only where **all three** hold. Any single\n160\tfailure files the row **correctness**.\n161\t\n162\t| # | Conjunct | Source |\n163\t|---|---|---|\n164\t| **T-1** | The row is **not** one of the five protected-core heads and is not a named dependency of a charter §5.2 blocking fixture. | DR-052 · N-11 · charter §5.2 |\n165\t| **T-2** | The row's output **gates nothing and feeds no number** — no other row's predicate reads it, no weight, strength, band, confidence or serving block derives from it. | charter G5's dead-cost logic inverted; §21.2 N-12 |\n166\t| **T-3** | Skipping it **cannot make any claim read as better-verified than it is** — the visible `SKIPPED-BY-BUDGET` mark is a *complete* account of what was lost. | OD-A-04's ruled ground: *\"a check which disappears under pressure is the dead-check class this pack exists to eliminate\"* |\n167\t\n168\t**A fourth filter, applied as a check rather than a conjunct: the nine honesty\n169\tsurfaces** (spec §12, `RULED(DR-048)`). A row that **produces** one of the nine —\n170\ttyped abstention badges (Q55) · per-node provenance (Q51) · defeaters as visible\n171\tfirst-class attacks (Q26) · STALE/UNDER-REVIEW (DR-015, Q18/Q58) · value markers\n172\tand reversal points (DR-017, Q50) · investigate-deeper (DR-045, Q34) ·\n173\tUNDER-EXPLORED (DR-016, Q62) · SKIPPED-BY-BUDGET and fallback labels (Q8/R7) ·\n174\tbuilds-on-previous disclosure (§17.6) — **is correctness**, because shedding it\n175\tdeletes a surface the reader is owed by ruling. No proposed enrichment row\n176\tproduces one.\n177\t\n178\t**The evidence grade of each conjunct is recorded, not blurred** (spec §3's\n179\t`R-AUTH-3` discipline). A conjunct may be satisfied by **RULED** text or by\n180\t**`CARRIED-DESIGN`** text, and the two are not interchangeable: `[CD]` is *\"still\n181\tbuild-ready\"* but is *\"flagged so a lens knows which sentences a ruling would not\n182\tdefend verbatim.\"* Every §4 filing therefore states which grade it stands on where\n183\tthe grade is anything other than RULED.\n184\t\n185\t**Across all 71 rows, exactly one conjunct on one row rests on `[CD]`: Q49's T-2**\n186\t(C-6 / C-7). It is marked in §4.9, argued at **CP-1**, and carried onto the\n187\tratification surface as **rider (d)** so that adopting it is a decision V takes\n188\trather than a reading V inherits. Q27's enrichment case rests on RULED text\n189\tthroughout.\n190\t\n191\t### 3.2 The protected-core map — where each of N-11's five heads lives\n192\t\n193\tRecorded so a reviewer can verify the **first** DONE-WHEN condition (*\"no\n194\tprotected-core row is classed enrichment\"*) by reading one table.\n195\t\n196\t| Protected-core head (N-11 · DR-052) | Owning row | Rows that carry or feed it — **all correctness** |\n197\t|---|---|---|\n198\t| **provenance** | **Q51** | Q8 (label rides *\"every node's provenance\"*) · Q15 · Q16 · Q22 · Q24 · Q60 · R4 · R7 |\n199\t| **abstention typing** | **Q55** | Q6 (the abstention price cell) · Q12 · Q17 · Q25 · Q56 · R3 |\n200\t| **standard-and-above blind verification** | **R5** | Q14 · Q31 · Q39 · Q40 · Q41 · Q42 · Q43 · R6 |\n201\t| **citation routes** | **Q16** | Q40 (reopen locators, exact-check preserved spans) |\n202\t| **serve-conformance** | **R9** | Q28 · Q44 · Q51 · Q52 · Q53 |\n203\t\n204\t**Every row named above is filed (a) CORRECTNESS in §4.** Neither proposed\n205\tenrichment row (Q27, Q49) appears in this table.\n206\t\n207\t---\n208\t\n209\t## 4. LRD-1 — which rows discharge it, and how the fixture becomes constructible\n354\t\n355\t**The dependency (`07-build-order.md` §7).** *\"Until at least one row is\n356\tclassified enrichment, the envelope's enrichment-skip terminal cannot fire and\n357\tcharter §5.2 row 6's fixture is unconstructible — a BLOCKING row.\"*\n358\t\n359\t### 5.1 What `FX-SRV-16` actually requires — stated before it is used\n360\t\n361\tQuoted, because an earlier draft of this package paraphrased it wrongly and the\n362\tcorrection changes the argument:\n363\t\n364\t> **`FX-SRV-16` — Condition marks reach the nodes they describe**, whose\n365\t> behaviour cell reads, verbatim: *\"an\n366\t> **answer-scoped mark's affected set** is stored **once** in\n367\t> `condition_mark_node` and **projected per node at read time** — an\n368\t> answer-scoped row with **no affected set** would project to the **empty set for\n369\t> every node**, showing `SKIPPED-BY-BUDGET` on the answer and on **none of the\n370\t> nodes it describes**, and would **silently fail `FX-C52-06` if the fixture\n371\t> inspects a node**.\"*\n372\t\n373\tThree consequences, and one non-consequence:\n374\t\n375\t- **`SKIPPED-BY-BUDGET` is an answer-scoped condition mark for every row.** There\n376\t  is no \"node-scoped mark\" in the contract, so **\"node-scoped versus\n377\t  answer-scoped\" is not the discriminator between candidate rows** and this\n378\t  package no longer uses it.\n379\t- **What the fixture needs is a non-empty *affected-node set*** — the set of\n380\t  nodes the skipped row's loss is attributable to, stored once and projected per\n381\t  node. `07-build-order.md` §7 states the same test operationally: *\"The fixture\n382\t  must inspect a node (`FX-SRV-16`) or an empty affected set passes silently.\"*\n383\t- **Whether a given row's budget skip yields a non-empty affected-node set is a\n384\t  data-model and fixture question this package does not own.** `FX-SRV-16`'s\n385\t  owning slice is **S9**, *\"reconciled per `07-build-order.md` §5.1 (PRE-01\n386\t  lane)\"*, with S5 owing the read-time projection half. **This package names the\n387\t  requirement and does not settle it** — see SP-C1 below.\n388\t- **The non-consequence:** `FX-SRV-16` establishes **no uniqueness claim about\n389\t  any row.** An earlier draft asserted Q27 was the only enrichment row that could\n390\t  satisfy the node-inspection limb; that claim was not established by the source\n391\t  and is **withdrawn**.\n392\t\n393\t### 5.2 The dischargers, named\n394\t\n395\t| Row | Candidate affected-node set on a budget skip | Role in LRD-1 |\n396\t|---|---|---|\n397\t| **Q27** (LLM · SPLIT) | The row's work is **per-node by construction** — DR-020 knob 8 mints the `UNCOVERED-SCOPE` note as **node text**, and the row is scoped to the split Q10 authorised, so the nodes whose scope coverage went unstated are enumerable from Q26's child set | **PRIMARY**, on scope-of-work grounds — not on a uniqueness claim |\n398\t| **Q49** (MACHINE · COMPOSE) | The fragility table is a composed-answer artifact whose reversal set **names components**, so the components the table would have covered are the candidate affected set | **SECONDARY.** Independently sufficient if its affected set resolves non-empty |\n399\t\n400\t**Both rows' affected sets are determined by the row's *scope of work* — the\n401\tnodes the row was owed over — not by the output the skip prevented.** That is the\n402\tonly reading under which any budget skip can have an affected set at all, since a\n403\tskipped row produces nothing to enumerate from. **Recorded as the reasoning, and\n404\tflagged at SP-C1 rather than settled here.**\n405\t\n406\t### 5.3 How `FX-C52-06` becomes constructible on ratification\n407\t\n408\tBoth halves, each with a named row:\n409\t\n410\t1. **The enrichment half.** A run whose envelope exhausts before SPLIT completes\n411\t   skips **Q27** under N-10's *\"typed enrichment skips first\"* limb; the answer\n412\t   carries a visible **`SKIPPED-BY-BUDGET`** mark (honesty surface 8) whose\n413\t   affected set is stored in `condition_mark_node` and **projects onto the split's\n414\t   nodes at read time**, so the fixture's node inspection sees it. **Q49 supplies\n415\t   the same shape at COMPOSE** and either row can carry the fixture.\n416\t2. **The protected-core half.** The same exhausted run reaches SERVE and **R9**\n417\t   (serve-conformance, N-11's fifth head) **refuses the skip**: the stranger test\n418\t   still blocks serving, and the run takes N-10's other limb — the hard stop\n419\t   serving already-verified components with `ENVELOPE_EXHAUSTED`. Any of Q51\n420\t   (provenance), Q55 (abstention typing), Q16 (citation routes) or R5 (blind\n421\t   verification) serves as the refusing row equally; **R9 is proposed as the\n422\t   fixture's named row** because it is the head DR-052 added and the one whose\n423\t   omission the ledger says *\"budget law permitted\"*.\n424\t\n425\t**The consequence recorded, so it is checkable.** On ratification the S15\n426\tacceptance bundle's **`UNCLASSIFIED` battery-row report should be empty** — all\n427\t71 rows carry a class — and charter §5.2 row 6 has a constructible fixture.\n428\t`07-build-order.md` §7's failure test is unchanged: *\"if it is non-empty **and**\n429\tno row is classified enrichment, charter §5.2 row 6 has no fixture and A4.4\n430\tblocks.\"*\n431\t\n432\t### 5.4 The brittleness, restated accurately\n433\t\n434\t> **Outcome — `DR-108`, 2026-08-07: NO PULL-OUT WAS TAKEN, so none of the three\n435\t> pull-out branches below occurred. `LRD-1` is DISCHARGED** on **Q27 + Q49**, in\n436\t> the ruling's own words: *\"**LRD-1 DISCHARGED** (charter §5.2 row 6's fixture\n437\t> constructible on Q27+Q49).\"* **The fourth bullet stands unchanged and is still\n438\t> live**: `SP-C1`, the affected-node-set derivation, is **owed by S9/S5** and was\n439\t> explicitly not settled by this sitting (§7's *\"what the yes does not decide\"*).\n440\t\n441\t**LRD-1's *existence* condition — at least one enrichment row — is discharged\n442\ttwice over (Q27 and Q49), so pulling either one alone leaves it discharged.**\n443\tThat is a stronger position than this package claimed in its first revision.\n444\t\n445\tWhat is **not** doubly secured is the fixture's **node-inspection limb**, because\n446\tneither row's affected-node set has been settled by the S9/S5 reconciliation\n447\t(SP-C1). The honest statement:\n448\t\n449\t- **If V pulls Q27 to correctness (CP-2)**, LRD-1's existence condition still\n450\t  holds on Q49 alone, and the fixture's node limb then rests entirely on Q49's\n451\t  affected set resolving non-empty.\n452\t- **If V pulls Q49 to correctness (CP-1)**, the same holds with Q27 alone.\n453\t- **If V pulls both**, LRD-1 is **undischarged** and charter §5.2 row 6 stays\n454\t  BLOCKING — V would need to move one of CP-3…CP-7 in the same sitting.\n455\t- **If neither affected set resolves non-empty at S9**, the fixture *\"silently\n456\t  fails\"* by `FX-SRV-16`'s own words — which is a **build-lane finding for S9/S5,\n457\t  not a classification defect**, and is why SP-C1 is recorded rather than assumed\n458\t  away.\n459\t\n460\t**SP-C1 · for the S9/S5 reconciliation, not for V's classification vote.** The\n461\taffected-node set of a `SKIPPED-BY-BUDGET` mark for a *skipped* row must be\n462\tderived from the row's scope of work, since the skip produced no output to\n463\tenumerate. `FX-SRV-16`'s owner (**S9**, reconciled per `07-build-order.md` §5.1,\n464\tPRE-01 lane) owes that derivation for whichever row the fixture uses. **This\n465\tpackage does not settle it and no classification here depends on the answer.**\n466\t\n467\t---\n468\t\n469\t## 6. The pull-out list — rows and readings this proposal judged contestable\n470\t\n471\t> **ALL TEN PULL-OUTS ARE SUBSUMED BY THE WHOLESALE RATIFICATION — `DR-108`,\n472\t> 2026-08-07.** V took the second of the two courses this section offered:\n473\t> *\"decline the pull-out and take all 71 as read.\"* **No pull-out was exercised\n474\t> and no row was ruled individually**, so **every CP below resolves to the class\n475\t> or reading §4 / §7 proposed** — which is also every CP's own recommendation.\n476\t> The section is kept **verbatim as the record of what V was shown before\n477\t> ruling**; DR-108 makes the point explicitly, that the promotion at rider (d)\n478\t> was *\"asked-and-answered: LRD-1 and C-6/C-7 defined for V before ruling.\"*\n479\t>\n480\t> | Pull-out | Subject | Proposed / recommended | Outcome under `DR-108` |\n481\t> |---|---|---|---|\n482\t> | **CP-1** | **Q49** | (b) ENRICHMENT, with rider (d) | **(b) ENRICHMENT** — and **rider (d) ratified**: C-6 / C-7 are **promoted from carried-design to ruled evidence** |\n483\t> | **CP-2** | **Q27** | (b) ENRICHMENT | **(b) ENRICHMENT** |\n484\t> | **CP-3** | **Q54** | (a) correctness | **(a) CORRECTNESS** |\n485\t> | **CP-4** | **Q58** | (a) correctness | **(a) CORRECTNESS** |\n486\t> | **CP-5** | **R8** | (a) correctness | **(a) CORRECTNESS** |\n487\t> | **CP-6** | **Q47** | (a) correctness | **(a) CORRECTNESS** |\n488\t> | **CP-7** | **Q30** | (a) correctness | **(a) CORRECTNESS** |\n489\t> | **CP-8** | **Q24** | (a) correctness | **(a) CORRECTNESS** — CP-10's rule supplies the limb answer |\n490\t> | **CP-9** | the *\"standard-and-above\"* qualifier | (a) tier governs activation only | **RATIFIED as reading (a)** — *\"standard-and-above governs activation, never class\"* |\n491\t> | **CP-10** | limb-split rows | (a) strictest limb governs | **RATIFIED as reading (a)** — *\"a limb-split row takes its strictest limb's class\"* |\n492\t>\n493\t> **Consequence for `LRD-1`:** because **neither** CP-1 nor CP-2 was pulled to\n494\t> correctness, the branch this section warned about — *\"If V takes (a) here **and**\n495\t> (a) at CP-1, LRD-1 is undischarged\"* — did not occur. **DR-108 records LRD-1 as\n496\t> DISCHARGED** on Q27 + Q49, exactly as §5.3 describes.\n497\t\n498\t**DR-061's courtesy, repeated** *(the text as it stood when V read it)*: these are\n499\toffered for pull-out from the wholesale ratification. V may rule any of them\n500\tindividually and ratify the rest with the batch, or decline the pull-out and take\n501\tall 71 as read. Each carries the DR-061 four-part shape.\n502\t\n503\t### CP-1 · **Q49** — proposed **(b) enrichment** · **its T-2 case is CARRIED-DESIGN, not RULED**\n504\t\n505\t> **SUBSUMED — `DR-108` (not pulled out). Outcome: (b) ENRICHMENT, and rider (d)\n506\t> is RATIFIED — C-6 / C-7's no-feedback contract is PROMOTED from carried-design\n507\t> to ruled evidence.** The authority defect this pull-out existed to expose was\n508\t> put to V and answered on the record (*\"asked-and-answered: LRD-1 and C-6/C-7\n509\t> defined for V before ruling\"*), so the promotion is a knowing act, which is the\n510\t> only condition this section attached to it.\n511\t\n512\t**What it is.** The fragility table: the reversal set and what would have to be\n513\tdropped or changed for the answer to flip.\n514\t**Why it matters — and the authority defect this pull-out exists to expose.**\n515\tT-1 and T-3 hold on ruled text. **T-2 does not.** The two sentences the\n516\tgates-nothing case rests on are both **`CARRIED-DESIGN`**, not ruled:\n517\t\n518\t- **C-6** `CARRIED-DESIGN` — *\"Fragility — the reversal set, and what would have\n519\t  to be dropped or changed for the answer to flip — is an **output**, served as a\n520\t  table.\"*\n521\t- **C-7 (forbidden by construction)** `CARRIED-DESIGN` — *\"Sensitivity may\n522\t  **never** feed back into base scores or arrow strengths.\"*\n523\t\n524\tSpec §3's own authority audit names this row in terms: the *\"feedback-loop …\n525\tcontract\"* on Q49 is one of the eleven `[CD]` clauses, and **DR-031 ratified\n526\t*\"classifications with named riders, not … full contracts\"*** — it ratified Q49's\n527\tMACHINE label, **not** output-never-a-weight. Spec §3 adds that `[CD]` text *\"is\n528\tstill build-ready … flagged so a lens knows which sentences a ruling would not\n529\tdefend verbatim\"*, and **no later DR promotes C-6 or C-7** (checked across the\n530\tfounding, ARCH-V3-R1 and PROG-V3-R1 ledgers). So classifying Q49 enrichment\n531\t**adopts the carried-design reading as part of the ratification** — which is a\n532\tthing V may legitimately do at a sitting, but must do knowingly.\n533\t\n534\tSecond, independent caveat: it is a **MACHINE** row, so classifying it enrichment\n535\tsheds **compute, not model calls** — its contribution to a call envelope is small.\n536\tThird: a reader could reasonably call a fragility table an honesty surface in\n537\tsubstance even though DR-048's canonical nine do not list it.\n538\t**Options.** (a) **Correctness** — the cautious reading; costs nothing in model\n539\tcalls, requires no carried-design adoption, and leaves **Q27 as the sole\n540\tenrichment row**. (b) **Enrichment, adopting C-6/C-7 as ruled by this sitting** —\n541\ta second discharger for LRD-1, at the price that fragility goes dark on the\n542\tbiggest runs and that a `[CD]` reading is promoted by ratification. (c)\n543\t**Enrichment, with C-6/C-7 left carried-design** — the class holds but the T-2\n544\tground stays flagged, so a later DR could unsettle it.\n545\t**Recommendation.** (b) — a second discharger materially de-risks LRD-1 and the\n546\tcarried-design promotion is narrow, explicit and rides the surface as rider (d).\n547\t**If V prefers not to promote `[CD]` text at this sitting, take (a)** and read\n548\tCP-2 immediately: Q27 then carries LRD-1 alone.\n549\t\n550\t### CP-2 · **Q27** — proposed **(b) enrichment** · **the strongest-authority enrichment row**\n551\t\n552\t> **SUBSUMED — `DR-108` (not pulled out). Outcome: (b) ENRICHMENT.** Q27 remains\n553\t> LRD-1's primary discharger, now alongside Q49 rather than alone.\n554\t\n555\t**What it is.** The plain-language uncovered-scope statement, minted as node text.\n556\t**Why it matters.** It is **the only row in the battery whose non-gating status is\n557\t*ruled* rather than derived**: DR-020 knob 8 ships it as a diagnostic note only,\n558\t`coverage_passed` is *\"a forbidden claim\"*, and `FX-DEF-02` keeps coverage-as-gate\n559\t**not shipped**. Unlike CP-1, its T-2 case needs no carried-design reading. Its\n560\twork is also per-node by construction, which is why §5.2 proposes it as the\n561\tfixture's row — **on scope-of-work grounds, not because any source makes it the\n562\tonly candidate** (that earlier claim is withdrawn; see §5.1). The counter-reading:\n563\tan absent coverage note could read as *\"nothing uncovered\"* — the answer is that\n564\tthe skip is never silent (honesty surface 8 makes the loss loud), which is the\n565\tentire mechanism that lets any row be enrichment.\n566\t**Options.** (a) **Correctness** — the safest reading of the coverage disclosure;\n567\tLRD-1's existence condition then rests on **Q49 alone**, whose T-2 ground is\n568\tcarried-design (CP-1), and the fixture's node limb rests on Q49's affected set.\n569\t(b) **Enrichment** — LRD-1 doubly discharged, coverage note sheddable with a\n570\tvisible mark, no carried-design adoption required for this row.\n571\t**Recommendation.** (b). **If V takes (a) here *and* (a) at CP-1, LRD-1 is\n572\tundischarged and charter §5.2 row 6 stays BLOCKING** — V would then need to move\n573\tone of CP-3…CP-7 in the same sitting.\n574\t\n575\t### CP-3 · **Q54** — proposed **(a) correctness**\n576\t\n577\t> **SUBSUMED — `DR-108` (not pulled out). Outcome: (a) CORRECTNESS.**\n578\t\n579\t**What it is.** Prior, posterior, delta and the moving evidence, served as a\n580\tnarrative.\n581\t**Why it matters.** The *facts* are event-sourced when the update is made, so a\n582\treasonable reading calls only the **narrative** sheddable. The counter: the row\n583\talso types `AMBIGUOUS_ATTRIBUTION`, and shedding it would silently drop the\n584\tmovement report on exactly the long runs where the most evidence arrived.\n585\t**Options.** (a) Correctness. (b) Enrichment — a third LRD-1 discharger, at the\n586\tcost above.\n587\t**Recommendation.** (a). === 400\n59\t        ? \"MALFORMED_REQUEST\"\n60\t        : statusCode === 403 ? \"MAKER_INVENTORY_UNSATISFIED\" : \"INTERNAL_ERROR\",\n61\t      message: knownError.message\n62\t    });\n63\t
